# Better-than-expected progress on risk control and digestion; raise price targets

We reduce our forecasts modestly to factor in some rise in industrial NPLs in 2025 due to our expectation of an effective supply side reform 2.0. We raise our price targets for banks on reduced concerns given good progress on risk control and digestion for LGFVs and property loans.

We expect slower asset growth but some NIM rebound in 2026 following modest pressures in 2024 and 2025. We continue to expect moderating loan and asset growth as regulators continue to reduce window guidance on loan growth and urge banks to reduce self-circulating loans. In addition, we expect more rational growth in industrial loans starting in 2025 to help reduce overcapacity risks. Specifically, we expect overall bank loan growth to slow from 9% in 2023 to 6.8% in 2025 and 7.1% in 2026 before some rebound in 2027. Positively, we expect some NIM rebound starting in 2H25, following some modest pressures in 1H25, to help digest industrial NPLs. Specifically, we believe slower overall credit growth will support loan yields and also reduce competition for deposits, which should also help banks reduce funding costs.

Modestly reducing our earnings forecasts for most banks as we factor in higher NPL formation as some industrial capacity optimization might start in 2025. We expect gross NPL formation to pick up to 1.57% in 2025 and 1.59% in 2026 from 1.41% in 2023. This should be driven by higher NPL formation in the industrial sector, partly offset by stable infra/LGFV credit quality and further moderation in property loan NPL formation. We expect banks to reduce NPL coverage ratio to cushion the earnings impact. We estimate that NPL coverage needs to decline 13-14bps on average in 2025 and 2026 to offset higher formation.

We maintain our Attractive industry view for banks with reduced concerns on risks, more sustainable long-term credit demand driven by manufacturing in China, and attractive dividends. Despite the likely modest earnings pressure, we believe more rational credit growth, reduced concerns about risks once the banks start to digest industrial NPLs, and some NIM rebound should support bank valuations, particularly if China can continue to show resilient industrial upgrades and further reduce reliance on the property market. Accordingly, we raise our target prices by 0% to 36% on reduced long-term concerns and still attractive dividend yields. Despite the recent rebound, China banks' valuations of 0.4x 2025 P/B (H share) and 0.55x 2025 P/B (A share) on average remain towards the low end of the ranges over the past 10 years. We believe the seasonal share price pullback before and after dividend payments should create a good buying opportunity.

# More rational credit growth with reduced window guidance, which should support NIM rebound

## We have seen a clear policy shift towards quality of credit growth rather than quantity

Policy has been shifting towards quality of credit growth instead of quantity, which is clearly stated in the 1Q24 Monetary Policy Report, including the emphasis of a decoupling trend between credit and economic growth in China, warnings against self-circulating credits purely for arbitrage and market-oriented structure of loans and deposits. This is also evidenced in the willingness to tolerate negative monthly TSF seen in April 2024 - see China Financials: April TSF: Shifting from quantity to quality (12 May 2024).

We believe this shift should lead to more rational credit growth, as low-quality selfcirculating credits are squeezed out and credit growth is driven more by the market rather than window guidance.

## We expect more rational growth in industrial loans starting in 2025, which will result in moderating total loan growth

As we have highlighted in our Regulatory Reset report of May 19, 2022, many policies in recent years have been designed to shift credit and resources back to the industrial sectors to maintain the competitive decentralized supply chain, including fiscal and tax policies, government and industry policies, as well as financial support. This has led to rapid investment and credit growth in the industrial sectors in the past three years, and capacity expansion has started to outpace industrial production demand in the past 12 months.

Exhibit 2: Pillars of industrial sector development
![](images/6e33d2a09233b9e12b06bad9bd5bfab02c3d89205212c350754fe3839f2d8ddf.jpg)
Source: Morgan Stanley Research

Exhibit 3: Manufacturing loan growth has picked up notably since 2020 and reached 23.8% in 2022
![](images/10ae61a4bac96b187f88f956c64ac528a86a867dbdbeb8ec92f3b69967cf828f.jpg)
Source: NFRA, PBOC, CEIC, Morgan Stanley Research

Exhibit 4: Overall manufacturing related credit, including trade finance, also remained elevated in the past two years
![](images/31f79941b7e077f73f02e244b6b85c4d012ac986f9655c66aac7102a37e8a487.jpg)
Source: NFRA, PBOC, CEIC, Trust Association, Morgan Stanley Research

Given the ongoing drag from property investment and local government infrastructure investment, it appears that policymakers and local governments are still pushing for a further pickup in industrial credit growth and industrial investment to support GDP growth in 2024. In addition, China is also focused on maintaining its competitive industrial supply chains with continued industrial upgrades. Nevertheless, we believe industrial investment and related credit growth will start to rationalize in 2025 for these reasons:

1. Drag on investment growth from property slowdown and LGFV risk control may ease in 2025, which could reduce local governments' incentive to push industrial investments.

2. Already lower rates on industrial credits, vs. the last cycle before 2016, will trigger a quicker slowdown in industrial credits when risks start to surface.

3. More private sector-driven investment in this cycle vs. more SOE-driven industrial investments before 2016 should trigger industrial credit slowdown after two years of negative PPI vs. the four years before 2016.

In this case, we expect total manufacturing-related credit growth to drop from 12-13% in 2023 and 2024 to below 7% in 2025, before rebounding slightly to above 7% in 2026 and 2027. We expect overall credit growth to decline to around 7% during the three years of risk digestion from 2025 to 2027 - see China's 3D Journey – Financials: Assessing the cost of supporting ongoing industrial upgrades (21 May 2024).

With the higher requirements on quality of investment projects, we believe overall infrastructure credit growth, especially that driven by LGFVs, should continue to moderate in 2024 and 2025, as the rationalization of LGFV debt started in late 2023. We expect the growth of infra-related credit to slow down to 6.1% in 2024 and 4.5% in 2025 from 9.5% in 2023, before rebounding to 5-6% in 2025 and 2026 – see China Financials: Policy shift has paid off earlier than expected (29 Apr 2024).

For the property sector, we expect the credit growth to stabilize at low single digits in 2024 and 2025; and to recover to mid- to high-single digits in 2026 and 2027 given the recent relaxation of home purchase restrictions and a rational policy combo (including lower mortgage down payment ratio, rates floor, and Rmb300bn relending quota) - see China Financials: New policy combo balancing property market support and risk control (17 May 2024).

Exhibit 5: We expect total credit growth to fall to 7% between 2025 and 2027 with more rational industrial credit
![](images/332c566994edcf1a61f8e7f0798a565eb93a1610f5d08d98b46a47b502b9f52e.jpg)
Source: PBOC, NAFR, e = Morgan Stanley Research estimates

## Positively, more rational credit growth should lead to NIM rebound with stable loan yields and lower funding costs

We expect some NIM rebound starting in 2H25, despite pressure in 1H25 due to repricing from the 25bps cut in five-year LPR in 1H24. Specifically, we believe the slower credit growth could support NIM from both the asset and liability sides: 1) Loan yield would stabilize or rebound with reduced window guidance and more rational pricing; and 2) Funding cost would be lower with reduced competition for deposits, as the pressure for banks to grow their funding base to support loan extensions is lower.

Source: NBS, CBIRC, CEIC,PBOC, Morgan Stanley Research

## NPL digestion – Property and LGFV well on track; while we expect industrial risk digestion to start in 2025

While we expect slower industrial investments starting in 2025, we believe further stabilization in the property market, more progress in LGFV risk control/SOE efficiency improvement, and continued growth in service and rural segments should support reasonable income and GDP growth. As a result, we believe stable infra/LGFV credit quality and further moderation in property loan NPL formation would cushion the pickup in industrial loan NPL formation and some deterioration in retail and SME loans during the industrial loan digestion period.

However, given our more conservative forecast on industrial sector NPL formation and higher industrial loan balance than property, we think overall NPL formation will still go up. Regarding LGFV, while the balance is larger, we estimate the NPL formation ratio is much lower at 0.6% in 2023 vs 1.9% and 2.8% for the industrial and property sectors, respectively.

## We expect some rise in industrial NPL formation with risk digestion starting in 2025

Over 2021 to 2023, we estimate that banks have been digesting 1.9% of manufacturingrelated loans as NPLs per year, based on the banks we cover. We expect that banks will start to digest a new round of industrial NPLs following the moderate industrial investments in 2025 - we estimate that the total portion of credit at risk could be \~10% within manufacturing credits by 2025, and banks need to digest 3.3% of manufacturingrelated NPLs per year, up from 1.9%. However, the rising industrial sector credit risks are still at levels more modest compared to the last round of supply-side reform between 2016 and 2020, when banks were digesting manufacturing NPLs at \~5% per year.

Positively, we do think the supply side reform 2.0 will have less of an impact on the financial system if the rationalization happens early, say in 2025, rather than prolonged overinvestment. If overinvestment lasts longer, the risks accumulated will be of a much bigger size than that before the last round of digestion in 2016, due to the growing base of manufacturing-related credit. While banks' profits may not be able to grow at a similar pace to help digest such risks given much lower interest rates.

Exhibit 6: We see a \~10% NPL ratio if China starts to rationalize industrial credit in 2025 with annual risk digestion equivalent to 3.3% of total manufacturing-related credit

Good progress on risk control and digestion for LGFV and

## property loans

Despite the higher market focus on property and LGFV loan risks, we believe property credit risks are manageable and will just take some time for the financial system to digest. LGFV risks also appear manageable as long as the future pace of credit growth can be contained.

## We have seen progress on improving SOE asset efficiency, which is key to a LGFV solution

We estimate that the overall government interest burden is still manageable at 2.3% in 2023 – see China's 3D Journey: China Financials: What a long-term LGFV solution could look like (15 Sep 2023). Effectively, our analysis shows that half of the interest payments from infrastructure projects can be covered by their own cash flows, and the rest could be better serviced in a variety of ways: improving efficiency, raising prices, swaps into government bonds, and/or lowering interest rates through negotiations or by new borrowings. Thus we highlighted that LGFV solution is not only debt control, but also efforts to improve SOE asset efficiency, of which we have seen signs of progress in our visits to six cities in China in 2024. We highlight that:

• In Zhengzhou, a coordinated process is in place to ensure LGFV interest payment and principal rollover – see China Financials – Trip takeaways: Coordinated LGFV risk prevention process in place (19 Apr 2024)

• In Zhejiang, we found further evidence that SOE asset base could be utilized to resolve LGFV issues – there are cases where the book value of government-related assets are undervalued (see China Financials – Trip takeaways: Divergence trend to continue, overcapacity key to watch (4 Mar 2024)).

Exhibit 8: We see various ways for governments to service the Rmb1.5tn in interest expenses not covered by infrastructure project EBIT

We think the peak of bank property NPL formation may be behind after

## years of risk digestion and supportive policies

Despite a rise in property NPL ratios to 4-5%, banks still cumulatively digested \~9% of property loans as NPLs from 2021 to 2023, per our estimate. With the gradual progress in project completion, we believe that completion is no longer a major drag on property sales. In addition, the government-led destocking efforts and policy combo should support project completion and help demand recovery, which should reduce risks for banks, in our view.

On the inventory digestion, we believe the risk control requirements for both financial firms and local SOEs (or LGFVs) will mean that local SOE purchases of available-for-sale inventory will be largely concentrated in the viable inventory in the major 70-cities. Our calculation shows that property-related NPL digestion by the financial system already reached around Rmb3trn covering around 70% of the dead inventory, with the remaining losses related to existing and new dead inventory to be written off over the next two to three years without affecting bank earnings as the annual digestion would be smaller compared to what has been digested by banks in the past. The relaxation of home purchase restrictions and decline in home prices to more reasonable levels, combined with healthier or even tight housing inventory levels in top cities could support a gradual rebound in both mortgage and property loan demand starting in late 2025 or 2026.

Specifically, we believe more downside support for LGFV debt could also reduce NPL formation in infrastructure loans, which we estimate to be at around 0.7% in 2023. We think property NPL formation could moderate from 3.2% in the past three years to 1.7% by 2025, releasing around Rmb200bn per year to absorb the industrial NPLs. Overall, banking sector gross NPL formation could pick up to 1.57% in 2025, 1.59% in 2026, and 1.6% in 2027, vs. around 1.4% in the past two years, based on our forecast.

# China banks remain attractive with healthy dividends and more sustainable credit demand in the long run

We continue to think that China banks can outperform with still healthy dividends, reduced concerns on risks from LGFV and property sectors, and stable earnings supported by more rational credit growth which is more sustainable in the long run and some NIM rebound likely in 2H25. While the focus shift to manufacturing could lead to a rise in NPL formation in the short term, we think it is cyclical by nature and banks will be able to digest the risks if the rationalization in capacity build-up could start in 2025. If China can continue to show resilient industrial upgrades and further reduce reliance on the property market, we think it will also drive more sustainable long-term credit growth, which will also support bank valuations. In addition, stable dividend payments should remain attractive for China banks, especially as some banks raised the dividend payout ratio with more focus on shareholder returns and some SOE banks are working actively on interim dividends plans.

China banks remain resilient and continue to outperform other sectors in terms of returns (including dividend payments) YTD. As of May 31, 2024, YTD return for the banking sector is 21.5% in the H-share market (vs. HIS of +7.7%), ranking it third among all sectors. In the A-share market, the banking sector has achieved a 19.6% return YTD, ranking it second among all sectors, much higher than CSI300 (up 4.3%).

SOE banks remain defensive with stable dividend payout and reduced window

## guidance

SOE banks have maintained stable dividend payout ratios at >30% for many years and indicated that they will keep the payout ratios sustainable in the future. The Big 4 SOE banks are also working on an interim dividend payout plan, which should help reduce the volatility in stock prices.

In addition, with reduced window guidance on credit growth, SOE banks should benefit the most from less competition, especially on loan pricing. This should support NIM recovery for SOE banks after 2 years of notable decline to support the real economy. On the deposit side, competition should also be milder resulting in lower funding costs at SOE banks.

Therefore, we think SOE banks should continue to see some improvement in valuations, even after the H shares of Big 4 SOE banks have increased over 20% YTD. We think SOE banks are still able to deliver positive profit growth in the following years and accelerate in the future as credit growth becomes rational and industrial NPLs are gradually digested. The current dividend yield of >7% for H shares could also attract more Southbound flows given a lack of other Wealth Management Products (WMPs) with high yield and low risks, which should also support the share prices.

## China Merchants Bank (H share) – Strong retail franchise should support retail loans and fee income

We think CMB will benefit more from the potential recovery in mortgage loans with the policy combo as discussed above, due to its large exposure to mortgages, \~20% of total loans as of end 2023. In addition, we see more opportunities for retail loan growth, especially personal SME loans and consumption loans, given the continued industrial upgrades and consumption recovery. These should lead to quicker loan growth at CMB than other banks without hurting margins too much as pricing competition would have reduced.

In addition, CMB's leading value adds from asset allocation service should also help the bank to get through the current various fee rate pressure and retain client AUM. While term deposits remain as the major products for retail customers, we believe they are gradually shifting towards WMPs for higher returns. CMB has maintained a competitive advantage in terms of product design, sticky client relationships and resilient AUM growth over the past years. We think CMB will lead fee income growth among other banks with comprehensive wealth management services once current fee rate pressure has been digested.

## Industrial Bank – Leading investment bank and wealth management franchise should support its revenue growth

We think Industrial is competitive in offering comprehensive solutions to corporate clients with a combination of corporate banking and investment banking services. Its industryfocused in-house research capability should help it to better identify opportunities and risks in different manufacturing sectors and supply chains. We think this should be the key for the bank's loan growth given more rational credit extension. We believe this should also benefit its leading investment banking business with market share expansion, which should bring high-yield loans and boost fee income.

In addition, AUM of the wealth management subsidiary has ranked no. 2 among all China banks, reaching Rmb2.26tn as of end 2023. We believe Industrial Bank will also benefit from households' preference shift towards WMPs, through comprehensive product offerings.

## Bank of Ningbo – Benefiting from SMEs, local credit recovery and WM business

We think Bank of Ningbo will continue to deliver double-digit ROE and >10% profit growth due to:

1) More SME opportunities riding on the industrial upgrades and still solid exports With industrial upgrades, we think credit demand from SMEs along the supply chain will remain solid. This will also lead to increasing personal SME credit demand. In addition, YTD export data remains healthy, which will continue to drive up credit growth in major industries in Zhejiang. With a comprehensive mechanism and service for SME clients, we believe Bank of Ningbo will continue to penetrate its home market in Zhejiang province and grow its market share in other regions with its nation-wide leasing and consumer finance franchise.

## 2) Strong recovery in local demand

We think local credit demand should remain healthy in Zhejiang province, with more business opportunities, more room for LGFV resolution, and quick recovery in households confidence.

## 3) Some acceleration in WM business as fee rate cut is largely finished

Bank of Ningbo is able to retain customers with retail AUM reaching Rmb987bn as of end 2023, up 23% YoY. With households' investment preference change towards WMPs, we think Bank of Ningbo is able to accelerate WM fee income via the offering of various products.

## Minsheng bank (H) – Reiterate as our Top Pick with potential ROE improvement in the long run amid its DNA transformation

As we highlighted before, we think Minsheng Bank is ready for ROE improvement after years of risk digestion under the new culture. The bank should also be a beneficiary of industrial upgrades as it has been investing more resources in product innovation and client service, and proactively looking to expand banking relationships from core strategic clients to numerous smaller companies along their supply chains. Notably, its SME loan balance increased 16% yoy as of end 2023, and further picked up 4% qoq to Rmb824bn in 1Q24. The supply chain client base is also enhanced continuously, with the number of online supply chain customers rising 25% YTD in 1Q24 to 23,999.

Exhibit 14: Minsheng Bank is able to expand to SMEs along the supply chains surrounding strategic clients
![](images/f28607f4938bdd03e664bf75fb20f3a378c350c1b47183bae3b495f93cde3b00.jpg)
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research

Exhibit 15: The number of supply chain customers online continued to pick up
![](images/f059fc95952a1f79206545339a390638fba3b06bdb45c119636eb08196b5acb0.jpg)
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research

We think major risks are behind it and the bank is able to digest risks from industrial sectors with continued improvement in asset quality. Minsheng's NPL ratio fell to 1.44% in 1Q24, from 1.48% in 4Q23. In the meantime, credit costs have declined since 2020, which we see as a good indicator for ROE improvement gradually.

Exhibit 16: Minsheng's NPL ratio dropped to 1.44% in 1Q24, from 1.48% in 4Q23

Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research

![](images/cd1837b06b55b39221d50131e692b90561144168d3a83f169c6ea7a7ce42b07a.jpg)
Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research

## Lower profit forecasts for China banks to factor in higher industrial NPL formation, partly offset by lower coverage ratios

We are reducing our forecasts modestly to factor in some rise in industrial NPLs in 2025 due to our expectation of an effective supply side reform 2.0, partly offset by lower coverage ratios. We expect NIM rebound starting from 2H25 following the rational credit growth especially in industrial sectors. This, along with moderate pressure on fee income, should result in gradual pickup in revenue throughout 2024 to 2026. However, the pickup in industrial NPLs should weigh on banks' profit starting from 2025, but we believe banks should lower their coverage ratios by 13-14bp in 2025 and 2026 to cushion the impact. In general, we reduce our earnings growth forecasts by 3.8% and 5% for 2024 and 2025, respectively, and introduce our forecasts for 2026.

We reduce NPAT forecasts for PAB by 12.5% and 18.2% for 2024 and 2025, respectively, as we factor in revenue pressure from both NII (due to de-risking efforts) and fees (from bancassurance fee rate cut), as well as higher provision for risk digestion during the transition period. We also cut earnings forecasts for CEB by 16.7% and 15.5% for 2024 and 2025, respectively, driven by higher provision due to limited room to cut coverage ratio given already low level compared to peers.

## We expect NIM rebound in 2H25, following some modest pressures in 1H25

We believe previous LPR cut and loan pricing competition will continue to weigh on banks NIM in 2024, but at a moderate level vs 2023. We expect an additional 11bp of NIM contraction in 2024, narrowing from 23bp contraction in 2023.

We expect NIM to remain largely flat in 2025 as we factor in some NIM rebound in 2H25, when banks to start to digest industrial NPLs. With the rationalization of loan growth, loan yields could recover and competition on deposits should also reduce, helping to stabilize NIM for risk digestion. We believe NIM will then gradually pick up from 2026 (i.e., up 4b on average in 2026) if no further LPR cuts, as policy makers have already realized the importance of keeping NIM at a reasonable level, and we think this would be essential to digest industrial risks and avoid systemic risks.

## Loan growth to slow to 8% in 2024e, and to \~6.5% in 2025e and 2026e, from 10.5% in 2023

We keep our 2024 loan growth forecast unchanged but reduce our 2025 loan growth forecast by 1.6% to factor in rationalization in industrial credits. In fact, we believe credit growth will moderate naturally, given the risk containment in LGFV sectors and slowdown in the property sectors. Bank of Ningbo and Bank of Chengdu could see double-digit loan growth in 2024-26, benefiting from better local credit demand. Loan growth at big SOE banks will also slow to 6.4-8% in 2024-26 with reduced window guidance, which should support NIM rebound with more rational loan pricing.

## the impact from fee rate cuts is finished

Despite weak fee income reported by China banks in 1Q24 following fee rate cuts, we expect moderate pressure in fee income growth in 2024 as the impact is gone - we forecast fee income to decline 2.4% yoy in 2024, down from a decline of 12.4% in 2023. For 2025 and 2026, we think fee income growth will continue to recover, at 3.5% and 4.6%, respectively. This is based on our assumption of: 1) some recovery in capital markets, which should drive up mutual fund sales and related agency fees. Although YTD mutual fund launches have remained muted, we think they should pick up if the capital market recovers; 2) rebound in credit card spending, as households re-gain confidence, which should drive up bank card fees; and 3) household financial assets shift towards equity products and WMPs, as households seek investment products other than passive deposits.

We expect non-interest income growth to slow down in 2024, dragged by fee income, but we still anticipate 2% yoy growth. This should be supported by investment income, following the similar trend what we saw in 1Q24 due to declining bond yields. For 2025 and 2026, we expect non-interest income growth to pick up to 4% and 4.7%, respectively.

Based on our new NIM, credit and fee income forecasts, we expect revenue growth to pick up to 1.5% in 2024, following a decline of 0.7% in 2023. Total revenue growth should then pick up to 5.9% and 8.2% in 2025 and 2026, respectively, on NIM stabilization. We keep 2024 revenue unchanged and lower 2025 revenue slightly by 1% with lower balance sheet growth and lower fee income. PPOP should follow a similar trend to revenue with a stable or slightly lower cost-income ratio as banks also focus on cost control. PSBC could potentially see more meaningful reduction in cost-income ratio if it is able to negotiate lower agency fee rates, resulting in quicker PPOP growth than revenue growth. Bank of Ningbo should deliver double digit growth in both revenue and PPOP.

## We factor higher NPL formation into our forecasts but also lower NPL coverage ratio to cushion the earnings impact

We expect gross NPL formation to pick up to 1.57% in 2025 and 1.59% in 2026 from 1.41% in 2023 for the banking sector, driven by industrial sectors partially offset by improved property market and stable infra. While we think the risks are manageable for banks, this will nevertheless lead to higher NPL ratios in 2025 and 2026, in our view - we believe NPL ratios will pick up 4bps per year in both 2025 and 2026 on average for banks we cover.

In light of the higher NPL balance, we think banks will also reduce NPL coverage ratio to cushion profit impact. We forecast 13-14bp yoy decline in banks' NPL coverage ratio in 2025 and 2026, while provision will still pick up 10.3% and 15% in 2025 and 2026, respectively, on higher NPL balance. Our credit cost forecasts were also raised by 6-7bp in 2025 and 2026 to reflect efforts to digest NPLs.

## Capital ratios to gradually improve throughout 2024-2026

We expect capital ratios for most banks will improve with rational RWA growth, despite lower ROE. SOE banks will need to meet the capital requirements by 2025 via TLAC bond issuance which is already underway. We expect 12-13bp pickup in Tier 1 ratio in 2025 and 2026, and 12bp pickup in CAR.

# Raising target prices for China banks on better-thanexpected risk control

Despite 4-5% earnings cuts for 2024-25e, we raise our price targets for China banks broadly due to lower discount rates and higher target P/B multiples. This is due to reduced concerns from the stabilization in the property sector, which should outweigh the deterioration in industrial credits, especially if the overcapacity issue could be addressed in 2025. We also factor in potential NIM rebound in 2H25 with more rational credit growth to help with industrial risk digestion. We thus cut our beta assumptions for most China banks, especially the SOE banks (with attractive dividend yield), quality shareholding banks (i.e. CMB, Industrial Bank), some turnaround names (i.e., Minsheng, CITIC Bank) and Ping An Bank to give it credit for its de-risking progress with good mechanism. We keep our long-term ROE assumptions unchanged for China banks. As a result, price targets for our covered banks are raised by \~20% on average.

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount methodology (DDM) to value China banks, and apply different base, bull and bear case scenarios. In our view, the DDM approach is a suitable valuation method given the steady dividend streams at China banks. We continue to apply 10% discount in deriving our target P/B for the H shares given the discrepancy between A share and H share valuations.

Our base case reflects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL formation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. We think more rational industrial investments should start in 2025 and banks will start to address the NPL risks accumulated in the last several years while the cost is still manageable. This would lead to higher NPL formation but also some NIM rebound with more rational competition. We assume the growth period of the second-stage DDM to be seven years and keep long-term ROE outlook and dividend payout ratio unchanged. Retail-oriented banks (CMB, PSBC) and leading city banks (Ningbo, Chengdu, Hangzhou) will still be able to deliver double digit ROE in the long-term. We think Ping An Bank is still able to achieve 11% ROE in the long run amid its de-risking process, thanks to its good mechanism during the transition period.

Source: Morgan Stanley Research estimates.

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. In this case, we think the risk digestion period could be shorter and support quicker recovery of industrial firms' profitability. Meanwhile, if global demand was strong, it should also lead to quicker credit growth for banks. We keep our assumptions on sustainable ROE and dividend payout at 10.4% and 49%, respectively, in our bull case to reflect the better outlook.

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. In this case, industrial firms' profitability could further deteriorate and systemic risks for the financial system would rise from mounting NPLs. We keep the sustainable ROE and dividend payout ratios at 7.0% and 24%, respectively, in this case with a shorter second-stage growth period.

We continue to apply weightings of 60%, 20% and 20% to our base, bear and bull

scenario values, respectively. Our discount rates vary from 10.1% to 18% in the base case.

## Risk Reward - Industrial and Commercial <sub>Risk Reward – Industrial and Commercial Bank of China (1398.HK)</sub>

(1398.HK)Well-positioned for earnings growth from strong balance sheet liquidity

## HK\$5.60PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a 3-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. The discount rate is 10.5% in the base case, and we assume 8.5% for secondstage and 8% for long-term ROE, with a 31% long-term dividend payout ratio. We use an exchange rate of 1.13 for RMB/HKD.

We apply a 10% discount in deriving our target P/B for the H-shares.

Consensus Price Target Distribution

Source: Re finitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

![](images/c507f11cb71881fcd662c048a031ffd2b366cfbe4e8a2873bf3088bda56e71d5.jpg)

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Good balance sheet mix and growth trend, providing support to a likely resilient NIM and healthy net interest income growth.

▪ Consistent improvement in asset quality.

▪ Emphasis on Fintech investment, providing more room for operating ef ciency improvement.

▪ Valuation looks attractive for ICBC-H at current levels, based on 2024E target P/B of 0.48x from a three-stage DDM model.

## Risk Reward Themes

Pricing Power: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research, Morgan Stanley Institutional Equities Division. The probabilities of our Bull, Base, and Bear case scenarios playing out were estimated with implied volatility data from the options market as of 4 fiJun 2024. All gures are approximate risk-neutral probabilities of the stock reaching beyond the scenario price in eithe three-months’ or one-years’ time. View explanation of Options Probabilities methodology here

## BULL CASE

HK\$8.30

## Implies 0.72x 2024E BPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 9.0% and 42%, respectively.

## BASE CASE

HK\$5.87

## Implies 0.51x 2024E BPS

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL formation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 8.0% and 31%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

HK\$2.03

## Implies 0.17x 2024E BPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Longterm ROE and dividend payout ratio of 6.0% and 13%, respectively.

Mean Morgan Stanley Estimates Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

![](images/859738733573d6f559e1a5ea8e205691ee466ab01a7b4202ea96fb36f077406a.jpg)

▪ Good balance sheet mix and growth trend, providing support to a likely resilient NIM and healthy net interest income growth. ▪ Consistent improvement in asset quality. ▪ Emphasis on Fintech investment, providing more room for operating ef ciency improvement. ▪ Valuation looks fair for ICBC-A at current levels, based on 2024E target P/B of 0.54x from a three-stage DDM model.

## Risk Reward - Agricultural Bank of Ch<sub>Risk Reward – Agricultural Bank of China Limited (1288.HK)</sub>

Play on government’s economic rebalancing

## HK\$4.40PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. The discount rate is 10.1% in the base case, and we assume 8.8% for second-stage and 7.9% for long-term ROE, with a 32% dividend payout ratio in the long run. We apply a 1.13 RMB/HKD exchange rate for our H share price target. We apply a 10% discount in deriving our target P/B for the H-shares.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Continuously improving asset quality, with the highest NPL coverage ratio among the Big 5 banks.

▪ Strong deposit franchise, especially in county/rural areas, resulting in a stable funding structure. Deposits accounted for 78% of total liabilities in F2023. ▪ Attractive valuation, with 2024E dividend yield of 7.7% compared to 5.3% for ABC-A.

![](images/824d433dc35e3d60f6081fd145140b061d9c065a3e55bc6468e2bde0eb24ef7a.jpg)

Risk Reward Themes Pricing Power: Ne<sub>g</sub>ative View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

HK\$6.59

## Implies 0.79x 2024E BPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 8.9% and 43%, respectively.

## BASE CASE

HK\$4.66

## Implies 0.56x 2024E BPS

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL formation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 7.9% and 32%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

HK\$1.55

## Implies 0.18x 2024E BPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Longterm ROE and dividend payout ratio of 5.9% and 14%, respectively.

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL flformation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 7.9% and 32%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

## Rmb1.52

## Implies 0.21x 2024E BPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Longterm ROE and dividend payout ratio of 5.9% and 14%, respectively.

## HK\$7.10PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a 3-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. The discount rate is 10.5% in the base case, and we assume 8.9% for secondstage and 8.1% for long-term ROE, with a 32% long-term dividend payout ratio. We apply a 1.13 RMB/HKD exchange rate for our H share price target.
We apply a 10% discount in deriving our target P/B for the H-shares.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Well-managed asset quality and solid positioning amid regulation restoration for long-term stability.

▪ CCB has a strong track record in bond trading to support non-interest income growth.

▪ Attractive valuation of 7.7% 2024E dividend yield for CCB-H compared to \~5.6% for CCB-A.

Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research, Morgan Stanley Institutional Equities Division. The probabilities of our Bull, Base, and Bear case scenarios playing out were estimated with implied volatility data from the options market as of 4 fiJun 2024. All gures are approximate risk-neutral probabilities of the stock reaching beyond the scenario price in eithe three-months’ or one-years’ time. View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

HK\$9.99

## Implies 0.70x 2024E BPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 9.1% and 43%, respectively.

## BASE CASE

HK\$7.68

## Implies 0.54x 2024E BPS

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL formation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 8.1% and 32%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

HK\$2.64

## Implies 0.19x 2024E BPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Longterm ROE and dividend payout ratio of 6.1% and 15%, respectively.

## Risk Reward - Bank of China L<sub>Risk Reward – Bank of China Limited (3988.HK)</sub>

Good progress with business transition and attractive valuation

## HK\$4.60PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. Key assumptions for base, bull and bear cases, respectively:
Second-stage ROE: 8.3%, 8.8%, 7.3%
Long-run ROE: 7.9%, 8.9%, 5.9%
Long-run dividend payout ratio: 32%, 43%, 15%
We apply a 1.13 RMB/HKD exchange rate for our H share price target.
We apply a 10% discount in deriving our target P/B for the H-shares.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We view BOC as a stable investment amid uncertainties on the investment growth and consumption recovery in China and lingering uncertainties globally in 2023. ▪ We see good progress in the transition of its wealth and asset management business. ▪ The H-shares offer an 6.9% 2024e dividend yield and attractive valuation.

![](images/d8fdea85013c570a05c389b0c479679a7d33d5a6515b60e4b5f5e10cf30a84f8.jpg)
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

Risk Reward Themes Secular Growth: Positive Technology Diffusion: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research, Morgan Stanley Institutional Equities Division. The probabilities of our Bull, Base, and Bear case scenarios playing out were estimated with implied volatility data from the options market as of 4 Jun 2024. All gures are approximate risk-neutral probabilities of the stock reaching beyond the scenario price in eithe three-months’ or one-years’ time. View explanation of Options Probabilities methodology here

## BULL CASE

HK\$6.77

## Implies 0.75x 2024E BPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 8.9% and 43%, respectively.

## BASE CASE

HK\$4.78

## Implies 0.53x 2024E BPS

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL formation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 7.9% and 32%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

HK\$1.65

## Implies 0.18x 2024E BPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Longterm ROE and dividend payout ratio of 5.9% and 15%, respectively.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ We view BOC as a stable investment amid uncertainties on the investment growth and consumption recovery in China and lingering uncertainties globally in 2023. ▪ We see good progress in the transition of its wealth and asset management business. ▪ However, we see limited upside potential for the A-shares given the relatively high A/H premium at current levels. Therefore, we are EW on BOC-A.

## Risk Reward - Bank of Commun<sub>Risk Reward – Bank of Communications (3328.HK)</sub>

Weak deposit franchise and more concerns on asset quality

## HK\$5.00PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. The discount rate is 11.3% in the base case, and we assume 7.7% for second-stage and 7.2% for long-term ROE with a 22% dividend payout ratio in the long run. We are using an exchange rate of 1.13 for RMB/HKD. We apply a 10% discount in deriving our target P/B for the H-shares.

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We believe BoCom may face more
downside risks to NIM due to weaker loan demand and rising funding competition in the Yangtze River Delta region.
▪ Its credit card business, one of its core strengths, has faced more notable asset quality pressure.
▪ Valuation is not compelling at the current level.

Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research, Morgan Stanley Institutional Equities Division. The probabilities of our Bull, Base, and Bear case scenarios playing out were estimated with implied volatility data from the options market as of 4 fiJun 2024. All gures are approximate risk-neutral probabilities of the stock reaching beyond the scenario price in eithe three-months’ or one-years’ time. View explanation of Options Probabilities methodology here

## Risk Reward Themes

Pricing Power: Ne<sub>g</sub>ative View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

HK\$8.97

## Implies 0.61x 2024E BVPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 8.2% and 35%, respectively.

## BASE CASE

HK\$4.65

## Implies 0.31x 2024E BVPS

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL formation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 7.2% and 22%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

HK\$1.92

## Implies 0.13x 2024E BVPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Longterm ROE and dividend payout ratio of 5.2% and 10%, respectively.

Noticeable reduction in policy intervention amid a rapid rebound in business fundamentals.

Social responsibilities continue to weigh in, coupled with a market downturn, eroding the bottom line.

Further economic slowdown could put pressure on credit quality and asset yield.

## Risk Reward - Postal Savings Bank of <sub>Risk Reward – Postal Savings Bank of China Co Ltd (1658.HK)</sub>

Strong retail base, products aligned with opportunities

## HK\$5.30PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. The discount rate is 13.5% in the base case, and we assume 9.9% for second-stage and 10.0% for long-term ROE with a 45% dividend payout ratio in the long run. We use an exchange rate of 1.13 for RMB/HKD.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ A stable retail funding base, which provides exibility to capture market opportunities.

▪ PSBC has avoided some higher-risk
business segments and maintained a clean balance sheet as a late market entrant.
▪ A leading position in rapidly growing
consumer lending markets and leading retail market share in higher-growth central China and county/rural regions.
▪ Low-hanging fruit in fee-based businesses, both corporate and retail.
▪ Valuations look attractive at current
levels, with ROE upside potential thanks to high provision buffer.

![](images/42f4c4fb935bddc1c8f70b10461a0b883e0459ce9187457184920fef8181a6de.jpg)
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research, Morgan Stanley Institutional Equities Division. The probabilities of our Bull, Base, and Bear case scenarios playing out were estimated with implied volatility data from the options market as of 4 fiJun 2024. All gures are approximate risk-neutral probabilities of the stock reaching beyond the scenario price in either three-months’ or one-years’ time. View explanation of Options Probabilities methodology here

## Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Positive

View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

HK\$7.91

## Implies 0.83x 2024E BVPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 11.5% and 53%, respectively.

## BASE CASE

## HK\$5.40

## Implies 0.56x 2024E BVPS

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL formation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 10.0% and 45%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

HK\$2.60

## Implies 0.27x 2024E BVPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Lower long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 8% and 33%, respectively.

![](images/34bc5f9848b4a5ee14d8bed95dfb471fef9bd4352497a80cb2b2da6c035ee187.jpg)

## RISKS TO PT/RATING

## RISKS TO UPSIDE

Noticeable reduction in policy intervention amid a rapid rebound in business fundamentals.

Evidence of better asset quality trend in consumer, micro and SME lending.

Improvement in operating ef ciency.

## RISKS TO DOWNSIDE

Long-term pro tability remaining low for <sup>fi</sup>PSBC's agency branch network client base.

More social responsibilities that lower fees for its agriculture-related or SME clients on policy guidance.

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. The probabilities of our Bull, Base, and Bear case scenarios playing out were estimated with implied volatility data from the options market as of 4 fiJun 2024. All gures are approximate risk-neutral probabilities of the stock reaching beyond the scenario price in eithe three-months’ or one-years’ time. View explanation of Options Probabilities methodology here

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Well-established retail franchise and strong brand name. Retail business contributed over \~60% of CMB's revenue in F2023.

▪ High transparency in WMP and investment NSCA.

▪ Superior risk management and prudent NPL recognition. Its NPL/overdue over 90 days ratio remains well above that of other shareholding banks. Its relatively high NPL coverage ratio also leaves room for a pro t growth buffer.

▪ Short-term pressure on fee income from fee rate cut and insurance sales. ▪ Valuations at current levels look attractive.

![](images/f17728ce7325ad605cb819d8c14be59279e9c84033f5da1e46b03a083f17c70e.jpg)
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Positive

View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## HK\$62.32

## Implies 1.36x 2024E BPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 15% and 65%, respectively.

## BASE CASE

## HK\$47.26

## Implies 1.03x 2024E BPS

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL formation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 13.2% and 58%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

## HK\$18.46

## Implies 0.4x 2024E BPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) lower long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 11% and 53%, respectively.

## RISKS TO PT/RATING

## RISKS TO UPSIDE

Faster-than-expected bond market development.

More resilient credit demand leading to stronger NIM.

Noticeable reduction in policy intervention.

## RISKS TO DOWNSIDE

Slower-than-expected retail AUM growth, resulting in less support for the sale of asset management products.

Worse-than-expected asset quality.

Slower build-up of regulatory system for asset management that delays consolidation.

A comprehensive ntech rm in the making

## Rmb13.60PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. Discount rate is 14.3% in the base case, and we assume 10.4% for second-stage and 11% long-term ROE with a 50% long-term dividend payout ratio.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Strengthening client franchise to support continued growth in wealth management business and funding base optimization.
▪ Short-term pressure on fee income from fee rate cut and insurance sales.
▪ More independent loan planning with less policy impact and prudent moderation of retail credit growth to a more rational level. ▪ Notably cleaned up balance sheet. Derisking efforts could add pressure on NIM outlook.
▪ Valuations look attractive at current
levels, as we believe PAB is on the right path to higher ROE among peers.

![](images/e05601c3c2cd9d88ddbb48eaaa761fc307b41482ded2eea6347c7945f0cab4f0.jpg)

## Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Positive

View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## implying 0.88x 2024E P/B

Rmb19.83

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 13.5% and 61%, respectively.

## BASE CASE

## Rmb13.40

## implying 0.60x 2024E P/B

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL flformation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 11% and 50%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

## Rmb7.73

## implying 0.34x 2024E P/B

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) lower long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 10.5% and 50%, respectively.

(0998.HK)Stable business growth with improved risk pro le and business restructure

## HK\$4.50PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a 3-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. The discount rate is 12.8% in the base case, and we assume 8.2% for secondstage and 7.1% long-term ROE, with a 24% long-term dividend payout ratio. We apply a 1.13 RMB/HKD exchange rate for our H-share price target. We also apply a 10% discount in deriving our target P/B for the H-shares.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Risk pro le has improved after years of risk digestion in focused areas, such as property market and credit cards. ▪ We believe the bank could see some business growth with its cleaner and healthier balance sheet, thanks to more rational asset expansion over the past several years.

▪ Business shifting to retail-focused should support revenue growth, as evidenced in healthy retail AUM and WMP balance growth.

▪ Current valuation looks fair with resumed growth potential, based on 2024E target P/B of 0.30x.

## BULL CASE

HK\$8.76

## Implies 0.59x 2024E BPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 8.4% and 39%, respectively.

## BASE CASE

## HK\$4.15

## Implies 0.28x 2024E BPS

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL formation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 7.1% and 24%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

HK\$1.36

## Implies 0.09x 2024E BPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) lower long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 4.8% and 6%, respectively.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ A complete DNA transformation with new client and product strategies to build a true client-focused commercial bank.

▪ Collaboration across the bank on better client services for a high-quality client base, supported by KPI system change and technology support (i.e., the adoption of Feishu).

▪ Legacy asset quality issue has been largely resolved after three years of extensive risk digestion.

▪ Potential pro t growth and continued ROE improvement from reduced NPL pressure, funding mix shift and even rebounding loan yield.

▪ Valuation for Minsheng-H looks attractive based on our target 2024E P/B of 0.3x.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ A complete DNA transformation with new client and product strategies to build a true client-focused commercial bank.

▪ Collaboration across the bank on better client services for a high-quality client base, supported by KPI system change and technology support (i.e., the adoption of Feishu).

▪ Legacy asset quality issue has been largely resolved after three years of extensive risk digestion.

▪ Potential pro t growth and continued ROE improvement from reduced NPL pressure, funding mix shift and even rebounding loan yield.

▪ We see valuation as fair for Minsheng-A at the current level, based on our target 2024E P/B of 0.33x.

## Risk Reward - China Everbright Ba<sub>Risk Reward – China Everbright Bank Co Ltd (6818.HK)</sub>

NIM compression and NPL digestion remain key to watch

## HK\$1.80PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. Discount rate is 18.0% in the base case, and we assume 7.1% for secondstage and 6.5% for long-term ROE with a 26% long-term dividend payout ratio. We apply a 1.13 RMB/HKD exchange rate for our H share price target.
We apply a 10% discount in deriving our target P/B for the H-shares.

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We expect NIM compression for Everbright, led by weaker loan demand, which should weigh on its asset yield more than peers'.

▪ Fee growth potential appears limited amid a lack of growth momentum.

▪ Retail business orientation and risk digestion is still in progress, which may take more time.

▪ Everbright-H valuation appears high at current levels based on our target 2024E P/B of 0.20x.

Noticeable reduction in policy intervention amid a rapid rebound in business fundamentals.

Faster-than-expected NIM contraction; worsethan-expected asset quality on loans to SMEs.

## Risk Reward - Hua Xia Ba<sub>Risk Reward – Hua Xia Bank (600015.SS)</sub>

Concerns around asset quality and weak deposit franchise

## Rmb4.00PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. The discount rate is 15.4% in the base case, and we assume 7.3% for second-stage and 6.5% for long-term ROE with a 15% dividend payout ratio in the long run.

![](images/57f158d3b76a89b8826a6b9a2947e80122ec987cdd2407b8b3d95a0962a4ddbd.jpg)

![](images/d6c8f6be230d9b0eeb535e6cd2fc5c79e3ae8165785f462db80eac0864dc24f4.jpg)
Key: Historical Stock Performance Current Stock Price Price Target
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Asset quality remains a notable concern with rising NPL ratios and credit costs. ▪ Downside pressure on its ROE is more notable than peers, in light of weaker loan demand and pricing power, in addition to funding pressure. ▪ Relatively low NPL coverage ratio of \~160%, which constrains Huaxia's ability to release provisions to support pro fit growth amid a challenging environment.

![](images/5c9e73d3c9c89784309c65ca4ee41992c60880fdb235e16e4526d2dd5b1cff3c.jpg)

## Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Ne<sub>g</sub>ative View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## Rmb8.49

## Implies 0.43x 2024E BVPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 7.5% and 31%, respectively

## BASE CASE

## Rmb3.23

## Implies 0.16x 2024E BVPS

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL flformation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 6.5% and 15%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

## Rmb1.63

## Implies 0.08x 2024E BVPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Longterm ROE and dividend payout ratio of 4.5% and 4%, respectively.

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ We believe SPDB will face more pressure to digest risk in 2025 and beyond, which could lead to higher credit costs than peers. ▪ Still on the way to transitioning its business, which could affect NIM and fee income growth. We think SPDB is more sensitive to ROE reduction than its peers due to weak provision coverage.

## Risk Reward Themes

Pricing Power: Ne<sub>g</sub>ative View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## Rmb9.70

## Implies 0.45x 2024E BPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE of 7.0% and dividend payout ratio of 43%.

## BASE CASE

## Implies 0.16x 2024E BPS

## Rmb3.56

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL flformation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 6.0% and 28%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

## Rmb1.18

## Implies 0.05x 2024E BPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Longterm ROE and dividend payout ratio of 4.0% and 8%, respectively.

## RISKS TO PT/RATING

## RISKS TO UPSIDE

Noticeable reduction in policy intervention amid a rapid rebound in business fundamentals.

Better-than-expected NIM and asset quality, higher growth in retail banking and higher contribution of fee income.

## RISKS TO DOWNSIDE

Social responsibilities continue to weigh in, coupled with a market downturn, eroding the bottom line.

Higher-than-expected credit costs and risk from the previous credit expansion.

Our preferred city bank investment

## Rmb34.30PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. The discount rate is 12.8% in the base case, and we assume 14.1% for second-stage and 14.0% for long-term ROE with a 61% dividend payout ratio in the long run. Our price target implies 2024E P/B of 1.14x.

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Rising market share in rapidly growing Zhejiang region should drive quality loan growth.

▪ Client-focused culture and strategy have helped the bank build a strong client franchise.

▪ The quality of this client franchise is evidenced in the bank's leading loan yield, superior funding cost and higher NIM. ▪ Strong ability in balance sheet expansion, with a unique asset mix and potential retail business expansion after the 1H22 acquisition of Huarong Consumer Financing. ▪ Strong asset quality control has kept its NPL ratio at a very low level across multiple business cycles over the past 15 years.

## BULL CASE

## Rmb42.93

## Implies 1.42x 2024E BPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 15% and 64%, respectively.

## BASE CASE

## Rmb37.91

## Implies 1.26x 2024E BPS

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL flformation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 14.0% and 61%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

## Rmb14.77

## Implies 0.49x 2024E BPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Longterm ROE and dividend payout ratio of 11% and 51%, respectively.

## Risk Reward - Bank of Chengdu <sub>Risk Reward – Bank of Chengdu Co Ltd (601838.SS)</sub>

At the center of Western China's development

## Rmb18.70PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. Key assumptions for base, bull, and bear:
Second-stage ROE: 13.9%, 15.1%, 13.4%
Long-run ROE: 12%, 14.5%, 11%
Long-run dividend payout ratio: 54%, 63%, 51%.

## Consensus Price Target Distribution

## OVERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ Western China's growth is more concentrated in the Chengdu region, which should bene <sup>fi</sup>t credit demand and loan growth at Bank of Chengdu.

▪ Localized development path leads to a wide range of local client resources, resulting in a strong corporate banking business.

▪ Fast-developing retail banking business with steadily improved retail deposit and loan mix, optimizing the bank's business structure and supporting its sustainable growth.

▪ Comprehensive and reasonable corporate government structure to ensure effective and steady implementation of strategies. ▪ Ef cient risk management and prudent <sup>fi</sup>assessment process leads to superior asset quality.

![](images/f3329650905ad43b4e3eab40c53ed022a79b357b03477be2f654effc3fe21abf.jpg)
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## Risk Reward Themes

Secular Growth: Positive View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

## implying 1.42x 2024E P/B

## Rmb28.55

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE of 14.5% and dividend payout ratio of 63%.

## BASE CASE

## Rmb18.22

## implying 0.91x 2024E P/B

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL formation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 12% and 54%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

## Rmb10.40

## implying 0.52x 2024E P/B

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Longterm ROE and dividend payout ratio of 11% and 51%, respectively.

## RISKS TO UPSIDE

Noticeable reduction in policy intervention and stronger-than-expected consumption activity recovery.

More resilient NIM trend than expected.

Better-than-expected loan yields.

## RISKS TO DOWNSIDE

Unexpected departure of chairman, who has built the risk and local client-focused culture at Bank of Chengdu

Weaker-than-expected macro trends that weigh on NIM and credit growth.

## Rmb13.20PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. The discount rate is 14.3% in the base case, and we assume 12.6% for second-stage and 11.5% long-term ROE with a 48% dividend payout ratio in the long run. Our price target implies 2024E P/B of 0.71x.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Low risk-taking strategy for both
corporate and retail loans targeting local SMEs and households. Infrastructure loans with high asset quality account for most corporate loans.
▪ Strong track record in retail business
transition, evidenced by notable retail loans expansion and positive retail shift in asset mix. Fee income growth is solid, driven by fast expansion of trust, investment banking and wealth management services.
▪ But the bank faces <sub>fi</sub>erce competition in deposits from major national banks in
Hangzhou, as re ected by higher-than-peers funding costs, weighing on NIM.
▪ Higher-than-peers RWA growth could put a strain on capital, weighing on the bank's loan growth.

![](images/c1eae392a51b4256d2b9d3e42da0c50dd58c5513c397d451ac215c0d59a2be22.jpg)

## Risk Reward Themes

Pricing Power: Ne<sub>g</sub>ative View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

Rmb17.47

## Implies 0.94x 2024E BPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) long-term ROE of 13% and dividend payout ratio of 58%.

## BASE CASE

## Rmb13.68

## Implies 0.74x 2024E BPS

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL flformation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 11.5% and 48%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

Rmb7.62

## Implies 0.41x 2024E BPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Longterm ROE and dividend payout ratio of 9.5% and 43%, respectively.

## Risk Reward – Bank of Hangzhou Co Ltd (600926.SS)

## Risk Reward - Bank of Beijing C<sub>Risk</sub> <sub>Reward</sub> <sub>–</sub> <sub>Bank</sub> <sub>of</sub> <sub>Beijing</sub> <sub>Co</sub> <sub>Ltd</sub> <sub>(601169.SS)</sub>

Good initiatives on retail transformation but risk digestion is a key concern

## Rmb5.20PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a three-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. The discount rate is 13.5% in the base case, and we assume 8.7% for second-stage and 9% for long-term ROE with a 39% dividend payout ratio in the long run. Our price target implies 2024E P/B of 0.41x.

## EQUAL-WEIGHT THESIS

▪ Headquartered in Beijing, Bank of Beijing appears well-positioned to capture the strategic growth opportunities and bene t from regional retail wealth accumulation. ▪ Strong track record in retail banking transformation as evidenced by notable AUM and client number accretion. ▪ That said, overhangs of NPL and interbank risk digestion remain key concerns. NIM compression led by asset de-risking and social responsibility on yield reduction could weigh on revenue.

## BASE CASE

## implying 0.43x 2024E P/B

## Rmb5.41

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL formation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE of 9% and dividend payout ratio of 39%.

## BEAR CASE

## Rmb2.48

## Risk Reward - Chongqing Rural Com<sub>Risk Reward – Chongqing Rural Commercial Bank (3618.HK)</sub>

Liquid balance sheet, but pressure on asset quality and ROE

## HK\$2.60PRICE TARGET

We continue to use a 3-stage dividend discount model, probability-weighted 60% base, 20% bull, 20% bear. The discount rate is 18% in the base case, and we assume 8.1% for secondstage and 7.5% for long-term ROE with a 27% long-term dividend payout ratio.

Consensus Price Target Distribution Source: Re finitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

![](images/ecd19cef8ede4101cb1bcc0974c293e8a0871503a9463d239d39523d03b9c6f0.jpg)

## RISK REWARD CHART

![](images/df4164f9bb492210e30ba307ecd99246d25faa2bd72deff3b2c3c73438d2762e.jpg)
Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## UNDERWEIGHT THESIS

▪ CRCB has a relatively liquid balance sheet among the China banks under our coverage. ▪ However, the regional economic
slowdown in Chongqing could weigh on the bank's asset quality and pressure the bank's ROE from 2024.
▪ We believe the valuation for CRCB is fair at the current level, based on a target
2024e P/B of 0.20x.

![](images/77ea44a27185f12f6937f235b9d23c0627a631fc81637bda0cfad57812c3c37d.jpg)

Risk Reward Themes Secular Growth: Ne<sub>g</sub>ative View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here

## BULL CASE

HK\$4.56

## Implies 0.35x 2024E BPS

Our bull case assumes better-than-expected risk digestion, quick recovery in the property market and strong global demand. 1) Resilient bank ROE in 2024 and 2025, driven by quicker NIM rebound; 2) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 8.5% and 39%, respectively.

## BASE CASE

## HK\$2.39

## Implies 0.18x 2024E BPS

Our base case re ects our outlook for moderating credit growth, higher NPL flformation from industrial sectors, as well as some NIM rebound in 2H25. 1) Loan growth to moderate with reduced window guidance; 2) NPL formation to pick up in 2025 and 2026 from industrial sectors; 3) Long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 7.5% and 27%, respectively.

## BEAR CASE

## HK\$1.03

## Implies 0.08x 2024E BPS

Our bear case assumes overinvestment in industrial sectors, negative PPI lasting for longer and weak global demand. 1) Banks NIM could be under pressure for longer; 2) Higher NPL formation in industrial sectors could weigh on banks' asset quality; 3) Lower long-term ROE and dividend payout ratio of 5.5% and 5%, respectively.

Mean Morgan Stanley Estimates Source: Re nitiv, Morgan Stanley Research

## RISKS TO PT/RATING

## RISKS TO UPSIDE

Noticeable reduction in policy intervention amid a rapid rebound in business fundamentals.

Expanding fee income contribution, especially from rapid wealth management business rampup.

## RISKS TO DOWNSIDE

Social responsibilities continue to weigh in, coupled with a market downturn, eroding bottom line.

Slowdown in Chongqing's economic growth.

## STOCK RATINGS

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell. Investors should carefully read the definitions of al ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone. In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice. An investor's decision to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations

## Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months

Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.

## Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.
